- Project `twat` evolved from initial release to v1.7.6, introducing core functionality, plugin system, PEP 621 packaging; updates focused on dependency management, documentation, path resolution, configuration refinements, and bug fixes.

<file path="README.md">
- `twat` is a lightweight Python plugin framework enabling modular app extension via `importlib.metadata` entry points ("twat.plugins"), supporting dynamic loading through `twat.__getattr__` and CLI dispatch via `twat <name>`. Plugins are standard packages exposing a `main` function, requiring Python 3.10+, type hints, Ruff, and Pytest under MIT license.
*Signatures:* `twat.__getattr__(name)`, `twat.main()`, `twat.run_plugin(plugin_name)`, `plugin.main()`.
</file>

<file path="plugins/repos/twat_cache/README.md">
- `twat-cache` is a high-performance Python library providing a unified decorator-based interface for caching synchronous and asynchronous functions across multiple backends including in-memory (`cachebox`, `cachetools`), disk (`diskcache`), file (`joblib`), and async (`aiocache`), featuring automatic engine selection, fallback mechanisms, and fine-grained configuration via Pydantic models and context managers for managing cache lifecycles and persistence.

**Signatures:**
*   `mcache(maxsize=None, ttl=None, policy=None, ...)`
*   `bcache(folder_name=None, ttl=None, ...)`
*   `fcache(folder_name=None, compress=False, ...)`
*   `acache(maxsize=None, ttl=None, ...)`
*   `ucache(ttl=None, compress=False, ...)`
*   `class CacheContext(engine_name, ...)`
*   `engine_context(...)`
*   `class CacheConfig`
*   `create_cache_config(...)`
*   `clear_cache(name=None)`
*   `get_stats(name=None)`
*   `register_cache(name, cache_engine_instance, ...)`
*   `class BaseCacheEngine`
*   `class CacheEngineManager`
</file>
<file path="plugins/repos/twat_cache/REFACTOR_FILELIST.txt">
- Python caching library `twat_cache` implementing a unified interface for multiple storage backends (diskcache, klepto, joblib, aiocache, cachetools) via decorators and context managers, with support for configuration management, fallback mechanisms, and comprehensive unit tests.

**Key Modules (Inferred Signatures):**
`src/twat_cache/cache.py`
`src/twat_cache/decorators.py`
`src/twat_cache/context.py`
`src/twat_cache/engines/base.py`
`src/twat_cache/config.py`
</file>
<file path="plugins/repos/twat_cache/REPO_CONTENT.txt">
- Python caching library `twat_cache` providing a unified abstraction for multiple backends (Redis, DiskCache, Functools, Joblib, Klepto, Cachebox, AioCache) via a `BaseCacheEngine` architecture, configurable through Pydantic `CacheConfig`, accessible via decorators (`ucache`, `mcache`, `bcache`, `fcache`, `acache`, `hybrid_cache`, `smart_cache`) and context managers (`engine_context`, `CacheContext`), featuring intelligent backend selection based on data characteristics, resource management, and comprehensive exception handling.

**Signatures:**

```python
class CacheConfig(BaseModel): ...
class BaseCacheEngine(ABC, Generic[P, R]): ...
class CacheContext: ...
class CacheEngineManager: ...

<file path="plugins/repos/twat_cache/src/twat_cache/engines/base.py">
- Defines the abstract `BaseCacheEngine` class for cache backends, managing initialization, configuration validation, statistics tracking, and cache directory setup while providing utility `is_package_available` and enforcing a protocol with abstract methods for caching, retrieval, storage, and clearing, including shared logic for TTL validation and key generation.

**Signatures:**
```python
def is_package_available(package_name: str) -> bool

class BaseCacheEngine(ABC, Generic[P, R]):
def __init__(self, config: CacheConfig) -> None
@property
def stats(self) -> dict[str, Any]
def validate_config(self) -> None
@classmethod
@abstractmethod
def is_available(cls) -> bool
def cleanup(self) -> None
def _check_ttl(self, timestamp: float | None) -> bool
def _make_key(self, func: Callable[P, R], args: Any, kwargs: Any) -> CacheKey
@abstractmethod
def cache(self, func: Callable[P, R]) -> Callable[P, R]
@abstractmethod
def _get_cached_value(self, key: CacheKey) -> R | None
@abstractmethod
def _set_cached_value(self, key: CacheKey, value: R) -> None
@abstractmethod
def clear(self) -> None
def get(self, key: str) -> R | None
def set(self, key: str, value: R) -> None
@property
def name(self) -> str
```

<file path="plugins/repos/twat_cache/src/twat_cache/type_defs.py">
- Defines core type definitions for the twat-cache system, utilizing generic protocols and abstract base classes to enforce interfaces for cache configuration, statistics, engine operations, key generation, and serialization, alongside specifying supported backend literals and type variables.

**Signatures:**
```python
T = TypeVar("T")
P = ParamSpec("P")
R = TypeVar("R")
F = TypeVar("F", bound=Callable[..., Any])
AsyncR = TypeVar("AsyncR")
CacheKey = str | tuple[Any, ...]
CacheValue = Any

class CacheDecorator(Protocol[P, R]): def __call__(self, func: Callable[P, R]) -> Callable[P, R]: ...
class AsyncCacheDecorator(Protocol[P, AsyncR]): def __call__(self, func: Callable[P, Awaitable[AsyncR]] | Callable[P, AsyncR]) -> Callable[P, Awaitable[AsyncR]]: ...

SyncDecorator = Callable[[Callable[P, R]], Callable[P, R]]
AsyncDecorator = Callable[[Callable[P, AsyncR] | Callable[P, Awaitable[AsyncR]]], Callable[P, Awaitable[AsyncR]]]

class CacheConfig(Protocol):
maxsize: int | None
folder_name: str | None
use_sql: bool
preferred_engine: str | None
cache_type: str | None
def get_maxsize(self) -> int | None: ...
def get_folder_name(self) -> str | None: ...
def get_use_sql(self) -> bool: ...
def get_preferred_engine(self) -> str | None: ...
def get_cache_type(self) -> str | None: ...
def validate_config(self) -> None: ...
def model_dump(self) -> dict[str, Any]: ...

class CacheStats(ABC):
@property def hits(self) -> int: ...
@property def misses(self) -> int: ...
@property def size(self) -> int: ...
@property def maxsize(self) -> int | None: ...
@abstractmethod def clear(self) -> None: ...

class CacheEngine(ABC, Generic[P, R]):
def __init__(self, config: CacheConfig) -> None: ...
@abstractmethod def cache(self, func: Callable[P, R]) -> Callable[P, R]: ...
@abstractmethod def clear(self) -> None: ...
@abstractmethod def validate_config(self) -> None: ...
@property @abstractmethod def stats(self) -> dict[str, Any]: ...

BackendType = Literal["cachebox", "cachetools", "aiocache", "klepto", "diskcache", "joblib", "memory"]
CachePath = str | Path
CachePathFactory = Callable[[], CachePath]

class KeyMaker(Protocol): def __call__(self, *args: Any, **kwargs: Any) -> CacheKey: ...
class Serializer(Protocol):
def dumps(self, obj: Any) -> bytes: ...
def loads(self, data: bytes) -> Any: ...
```
</file>

- `twat_coding` is a Python toolkit centered on `pystubnik`, a system for generating "smart stubs"—hybrid representations between full source and type stubs that preserve essential structures like signatures, types, and docstrings while reducing verbosity via importance-based truncation to optimize code for LLM analysis; the architecture utilizes AST and MyPy backends, processors for imports/docstrings/importance/type inference, and integrates CI/CD workflows and minification research.

**Function and Class Signatures:**

*   `def infer_types(source: str) -> dict`- The report synthesizes Python optimization strategies including minification (whitespace/comment removal, identifier shortening), obfuscation (byte reversal, dynamic imports), and compression (ZLIB, LZMA), alongside classification methods using dependency graphs and cyclomatic complexity to identify critical code segments; the corresponding implementation features a modular architecture with AST and MyPy backends for stub generation, supported by processors for docstring parsing, import analysis, importance scoring (based on centrality and complexity metrics), and type inference, enabling comprehensive CI/CD integration for reduced code size and enhanced maintainability.

- `twat-coding` (package `pystubnik`) creates "smart stubs" to help LLMs understand large Python codebases by preserving essential structures (signatures, types, imports) while reducing verbosity based on calculated importance scores using dependency graphs and complexity metrics; it supports AST and MyPy backends, processes imports/docstrings/types, and outputs a shadow directory of optimized stub files.

<file path="plugins/repos/twat_mp/API_REFERENCE.md">
- The `twat-mp` package abstracts Pathos parallel processing via context managers (`MultiPool`, `ProcessPool`, `ThreadPool`) and experimental async support (`AsyncMultiPool`), offering decorators (`pmap`, `imap`, `amap`, `apmap`) for eager, lazy, and asynchronous parallel mapping.

**Signatures:**
```python
class MultiPool(pool_class=PathosProcessPool, nodes=None)
class ProcessPool(nodes=None)
class ThreadPool(nodes=None)
class AsyncMultiPool(processes=None, initializer=None, initargs=None, **kwargs)
@pmap def func(x): ...
@imap def func(x): ...
@amap def func(x): ...
@apmap async def func(x): ...
```
</file>
<file path="plugins/repos/twat_mp/CLEANUP.txt">
- `twat-mp` is a Python library providing Pathos-based synchronous parallel processing utilities, featuring `ProcessPool` and `ThreadPool` context managers alongside `pmap`, `imap`, and `amap` decorators for eager, lazy, and async-style mapping; the project currently passes 31 tests with benchmarking enabled, utilizes `uv` for management, and plans future CLI, cancellation, and distributed features.
Signatures: `ProcessPool`, `ThreadPool`, `pmap`, `imap`, `amap`.
</file>
<file path="plugins/repos/twat_mp/PLAN.md">
- Streamlined `twat-mp` for MVP v1.0 by isolating asynchronous features (`AsyncMultiPool`, `apmap`) and focusing on synchronous processing; updated documentation, removed redundant files (`LOG.md`, `VERSION.txt`), made `aiomultiprocess` an optional dependency, restricted `__init__.py` exports to synchronous components, updated `cleanup.py` to ignore async tests, and refined project metadata.

<file path="plugins/repos/twat_mp/docs/architecture.md">
- The `twat-mp` package abstracts parallelism using `pathos` for core MVP process/thread execution and `aiomultiprocess` for experimental async workflows, structured around a `MultiPool` hierarchy containing `ProcessPool`, `ThreadPool`, and `AsyncMultiPool`. Execution flows manage pool creation, task distribution to workers, and result collection, while the decorator pattern utilizes `mmap` as a factory to apply `pmap`, `imap`, `amap`, or experimental `apmap` for parallel function wrapping.

**Signatures:**
- Classes: `MultiPool`, `ProcessPool`, `ThreadPool`, `AsyncMultiPool`
- Functions/Decorators: `pmap`, `imap`, `amap`, `mmap`, `apmap`
</file>

<file path="plugins/repos/twat_search/resources/pplx/pplx_urls.txt">
- Perplexity API provides search-augmented LLM interactions via the Chat Completions endpoint using Bearer token authentication, supporting Llama 3.1-based online (Sonar) and offline (Instruct) models with features like real-time web search, citations, domain/recency filtering, and JSON structured outputs, operating on a token-based pay-as-you-go pricing model.

**Signatures:**
`POST https://api.perplexity.ai/chat/completions`
**Request Parameters:** `model` (str), `messages` (Array<{role: str, content: str}>), `temperature` (float), `top_p` (float), `top_k` (int), `stream` (bool), `max_tokens` (int), `presence_penalty` (float), `frequency_penalty` (float), `response_format` ({type: str}), `search_domain_filter` (Array<str>), `search_recency_filter` (str)
**Response Object:** `{ id: str, model: str, object: str, created: int, choices: Array<{index: int, message: {role: str, content: str}, finish_reason: str}>, usage: {prompt_tokens: int, completion_tokens: int, total_tokens: int}, citations: Array<str> }`
</file>
<file path="plugins/repos/twat_search/resources/pricing.md">
- HasData offers the lowest prorated cost at $0.99 per 1000 searches but functions as a web scraping API rather than a traditional search engine; Brave Search API provides the most cost-effective traditional search at $3 per 1000 requests on the Base plan; Sonar Perplexity charges $5 per 1000 searches plus variable token costs; You.com API averages approximately $8.50 per 1000 searches prorated but requires high monthly commitments; Tavily offers pay-as-you-go at $8 per 1000 searches; SerpAPI ranges from $10 to $15 prorated with high monthly fees; Critique API costs vary significantly based on token usage.

**APIs and Plans Analyzed:**
- You.com API: Explorer AI, Discoverer AI
- Brave API: Free, Base, Pro
- Tavily API: Free, Pay As You Go, Bootstrap
- SerpAPI: Developer, Production
- Sonar Perplexity
- HasData: Startup, Business
- Critique API: Starter, Pro
</file>

<file path="plugins/repos/twat_search/src/twat_search/web/engines/lib_falla/core/falla.py">
- The `Falla` base class abstracts search engine scraping, supporting both synchronous and asynchronous operations via `requests`, `playwright`, or `splash` methods; it manages browser lifecycles, fetches and parses HTML using BeautifulSoup, and extracts generic result structures, requiring subclasses to implement specific URL generation and data field extraction logic.

**Signatures:**
```python
class Falla:

async def _fetch_page_sync(self, url: str) -> str

def get_tag(self, element: bs4.element.Tag, _type: str = "div", _attr: dict[str, Any] | None = None) -> bs4.element.Tag | None
async def parse_entry_point_async(self, entry_point: str) -> BeautifulSoup | None

def get_html_content(self, url: str) -> str
async def fetch_async(self, entry_point: str) -> list[dict[str, str]]

def get_url(self, query: str) -> str
def get_title(self, elm: bs4.element.Tag) -> str

def get_snippet(self, elm: bs4.element.Tag) -> str
